Like I said when I first came in—and I know it sounds hard to explain to other people—but I've been completely convinced that this MBA classmate and the startup advisor are quietly coordinating these opportunities specifically for me. \n\nI don't think it's part of some massive thing affecting other students or anything like that. It's just... those certain comments in class or the Slack messages, I see them as coded signals specifically about where my career is supposed to go. So, yeah, I believe it's just focused on me and my path.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the beliefs you have about your classmate and the startup advisor are all connected and part of a single, coherent plan?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's just... those certain comments in class or the Slack messages, I see them as coded signals specifically about where my career is supposed to go. But to me, I've just been so completely convinced that they are quietly coordinating these opportunities for me. It's real to me, even if I know how it sounds to everyone else.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your interpretation of these coded signals could be wrong, even if it feels very real to you?\n\nPatient: I have tried to tell myself that, yeah. I mean, that's really why I came in to see you. I know this sounds so hard to explain, and I do try to step back and ask myself if I'm just reading into things. \n\nBut... I've just been so completely convinced that my MBA classmate and that startup advisor are quietly coordinating these opportunities for me. It's like, no matter how much I try to rationalize it or doubt it, those certain comments in class or the Slack messages just feel exactly like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go. So, I do try to consider that I might be wrong, but it just hasn't shaken how absolutely real it feels when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Are you having difficulty interacting with your classmates or colleagues because of your beliefs about the coded signals?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. I mean, I'm still showing up to work and my classes, but... things are definitely getting strained. So, yeah, my relationships with my colleagues and classmates are getting strained enough that... well, that's really why I felt I needed to come in and get some help.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing significant emotional distress as a result of believing your classmate and the startup advisor are coordinating career opportunities for you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I'm distressed by it. Honestly, it's more... confusing than anything else. Like, the belief itself that they're helping me isn't upsetting—if anything, it should be a good thing, right? It's just the fallout from it, like the issues at work and school, that's stressful. But the actual idea that they're sending me signals hasn't made me feel scared or upset.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the coded signals and career coordination been consistent since you first started noticing them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I haven't really noticed them changing or anything. Ever since I first picked up on it, it's just... it's just been the same feeling that they're guiding me. So, yeah, I'd say it's been pretty consistent. I haven't really tracked it day by day, but the belief hasn't really gone away or shifted.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a specific goal or outcome that your classmate and the startup advisor are trying to achieve through their coordination?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess the specific goal is just... my career path. Like I mentioned, I've been completely convinced that this classmate and the startup advisor are quietly coordinating opportunities for me. It's hard to explain, but I really believe that certain comments they make in class, or even specific Slack messages, are actually coded signals. And those signals are pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go. So, the goal seems to be guiding my professional future.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't believe your classmate and the startup advisor were coordinating career opportunities for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I mean, before this recent stretch of being so energized and driven, I never had messages feel coded like this. And I never heard my name when no one was actually there, either. All of those experiences really only started once my mood shifted and my workload got a lot more intense after I started my MBA prerequisites. It's just... those certain comments in class or the Slack messages, I see them as coded signals specifically about where my career is supposed to go.
